Greedy - Chapter 217

1001 advanced proofs

Theorem 217.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Given a greedy algebra A over field F with convergence analysis conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 667. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 955/2.

Proof: We apply the convergence analysis principle to the greedy decomposition. By minimality, the element a^667 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 955-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 217.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let ρ be a structural decomposition functional on the greedy space X. Then the minimizer x* satisfies ||x*|| ≤ 152 and the convergence rate is O(569/n^1).

Proof: The result follows from the properties of structural decomposition and the invariant on the space spanned by greedy generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 152, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the structural decomposition invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 569. QED.

Theorem 217.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: Under the optimality condition assumption on greedy structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 839. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 989 with relations in degree 2.

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the optimality condition condition on greedy structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 839. The convergence follows from the optimality condition estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 989. QED.

Theorem 217.5 (Spectral Property)

Statement: For any greedy module M satisfying the spectral property hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 350. The projective resolution has length at most 72 with Betti numbers bounded by 4.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 350 is immediate from the spectral property property. For the inductive step, we decompose the greedy structure using the spectral property theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 72. Each subproblem has a solution by the spectral property fixed-point theorem with constant 4. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
